## 2026-10-20 script preview per-slot concurrency identity

The user-reviewed script Preview previously bound only CREATE/UNCHANGED totals. In an isolated PostgreSQL red test, a two-day preview showed Monday CREATE / Tuesday UNCHANGED; another administrator then added Monday's identical row and removed Tuesday's. Totals stayed 1/1, so the old Commit created Tuesday and an audit despite the changed reviewed write set. The new preview returns an opaque hash of each proposed employee/date/time/status plus any existing Availability row ID/version. Commit binds that hash to its preview identity and, under the store/employee transaction locks, rechecks the exact per-slot state before any Availability or audit write. It returns 409 if the reviewed state changed; exact-source idempotent replay remains supported.

The focused route/isolated database suite passed 83/83 after the red reproduction. Full backend isolated PostgreSQL passed **27 files/203 tests** on `codex_roster_backend_test_1792493211477_436c`; a subsequent fresh-schema 7/7 integration rerun also verified that replacing an unchanged row with the same clocks but a new row identity returns 409. Frontend passed **36 files/234 tests**, ESLint and production build passed; backend emitted TypeScript compile and Prisma validate passed. Fresh paired disposable Chromium/PostgreSQL then reproduced the swapped 1/1 statuses: stale Commit returned 409, retained only Monday's MANUAL row, wrote no import audit, and preserved 3 Shifts/1 publication. Re-preview and Commit created Tuesday's SCRIPT row and one APPEND audit; Week employee-row Calendar displayed both days. A separate fresh fixture reran the two-employee import and Calendar integration, including Cancel zero-write and stale revision/config guards. All exact QA listeners/schemas were cleaned. BHS tabs remain at login; BHS Submit semantics and real store acceptance remain unverified. No migration or Evaluate source/data change.

## 2026-10-20 script shortcut load failure recovery

The BHS Chrome tabs still point to `/login`. Auditing Molly's Staff script dialog found that a failed first GET for the current store's shortcut configuration left BHS Hurstville reference defaults visible and permitted a confirmation/save flow despite the actual saved store config being unknown. A new disposable Chromium regression first failed because the dialog had no retry control. The dialog now disables script editing, shortcut changes, Save, Convert and Commit after that load error; it explains the failure and offers an in-place retry. The retry reloads the current store config and requires the user to reconfirm it.

Current-source Chromium with paired disposable Evaluate/Roster schemas injected 503 for the StrictMode initial shortcut GETs, then used Retry. It recovered the saved test-store `AA 11:00–21:00` instead of the reference `10:00–22:00`, previewed `HandoverEmployee006<TAB>1AA`, and cancelled with **zero shortcut/import writes**. Guarded PostgreSQL found 0 Availability rows, 0 import audits, and the fixture's 3 Shifts/1 publication intact. Frontend 36 files/234 tests, ESLint, production build, Python compile and source diff checks passed. Exact temporary services/schemas were cleaned. This is a Molly failure-recovery rule, not a BHS retry-parity claim; real BHS Submit semantics and target store acceptance remain open. No backend/Evaluate source, migration or real data changed.

## 2026-10-20 UNKNOWN Availability template consistency

Following the direct Availability guard, audit found the template create/update route still accepted `UNKNOWN` plus a timed slot, or duplicate `UNKNOWN` slots, on one day. ## 2026-10-20 script import in-flight configuration version guard

The previous goal turn made progress by fixing an unrelated previous-day Shift false conflict and verifying rollback/concurrent duplicate handling. A new isolated PostgreSQL test reproduced an in-flight race: if a manager changed saved script shortcuts after the commit route read them but before its transaction acquired the store lock, the old service still committed one Availability row. `commitPresetTimeSlots` now receives the route-read shortcut and store-settings versions and rechecks both inside the locked transaction before any import mutation. A stale version returns 409 without a row or audit; the route forwards the versions. This narrows the cross-request race without changing script grammar or BHS assumptions.

## 2026-10-17 durable new-Shift request identity

Replaced the prior preflight-ID heuristic with a browser-generated UUID sent as `clientShiftId` on Calendar's atomic create-with-period API. The UUID is the new Shift ID; a new Roster-only `shift_create_requests` ledger stores request owner, store and payload hash in the same PostgreSQL transaction. A same-account identical replay returns the one existing Shift without bumping its period version; altered content/account is 409 `CREATE_REPLAY_CONFLICT`; after deletion the ledger remains and the old request is 409 `CREATE_REPLAY_GONE`. Calendar retains one ID across an uncertain retry, changes it when form content changes, and checks that exact ID after an unreadable result. This removes attribution to another administrator's identical Shift and prevents a delayed retry from recreating a deleted Shift. Evaluate's database and application were not modified.

Added migration `20261017010000_shift_create_request_ledger`. Before deploying to local `molly_roster`, created and validated a 76,358-byte pg_dump archive at `%TEMP%/molly-roster-pre-shift-ledger-20261017-0042.dump`. Prisma status now shows all 7 migrations applied; local read-only counts after deployment: 15 Shifts, zero request-ledger rows. Normal 4020 was rebuilt/restarted from current source and health reports database/Evaluate true; 5176 returns 200. A background Start-Process launch was rejected by automatic command review, so the backend runs in the current `npm run dev` PTY session instead.

Fresh disposable real PostgreSQL integration passed 3/3 focused create tests, including two concurrent same-ID creates, replay, changed payload/account, and replay after deletion. Full backend passed 22 files/152 tests with `--testTimeout 30000`; the first run under simultaneous frontend test/lint/build load had seven 5-second timeouts and no assertion failures. Frontend passed 33 files/212 tests, ESLint and production build; backend Prisma validate/build passed. Paired disposable Chromium verified committed 201 with broken JSON recovered the exact new Shift, another administrator's identical Shift did not falsely close Copy, and a failed request retried with the same ID then replayed without duplication. Temporary services and schemas were cleaned. Direct BHS and real STORE_EVALUATOR acceptance remain open.

## 2026-10-16 backend formal-role alias closure

Found a frontend/backend authorization mismatch: when one Evaluate job had both a store-specific Roster role and an older global role with the same code, the client selected only the store-specific role but backend `matchFormalRoles` admitted both IDs for Shift/template writes, Copy, Publish and Batch. Backend matching now reserves code matches across the current store job catalog, prefers store-specific roles, then applies name fallback only to unmatched jobs, selecting one role per job. Direct Shift/template validation uses that same map. Role reactivation/update must keep the selected formal job name; an API request cannot rename a matching code to an arbitrary local name. Existing rows and publication snapshots were not rewritten.

## 2026-10-09 paginated workforce integrity

Focused routes passed 35/35; the full database-enabled backend suite passed 94/94 and TypeScript checking passed. Evaluate and Roster still do not share a transaction, so a change after the last workforce read remains a documented race. The normal 4020 process has not been reloaded.

## 2026-10-15 continuation: serialized role and store-setting writes
Audited publish semantics before editing: existing publicationHistory PostgreSQL test deliberately republishes a reopened Shift with an inactive Shift Role and verifies both immutable revisions. This may be a real store policy choice, so no publication rule was changed; user clarification is pending. Separately found an unambiguous TOCTOU risk: create/update/batch preflight read Shift Role and settings outside the store transaction, while role edits did not take the store advisory lock. updateRole now takes that lock; createShift, createShiftWithPeriod, updateShift and batchUpdate re-read current active role after acquiring it; create/update also revalidate current settings before writing and use current role name/color snapshots. A disposable PostgreSQL concurrency test held the store lock, let create preflight pass, changed the role or opening time while create waited, then released the lock. Both writes rejected with INACTIVE_SHIFT_ROLE or OUTSIDE_OPERATING_HOURS, zero Shifts and unchanged period version. A second wait showed updateRole itself cannot deactivate until the lock is released. Full isolated backend 19 files/118 tests and TypeScript no-emit/production emit passed. Exact schema cleaned; normal 4020 health remains database/evaluateMolly=true. No schema, frontend or Evaluate edit. This backend race has direct PostgreSQL evidence; no new browser gesture was used. Internal pilot remains unaccepted pending target real evaluator, BHS behavior, sustained device performance and publication policy clarification.

## 2026-10-27 main Roster authentication response-order checkpoint

The main frontend authentication audit reproduced two response-order defects with delayed promises: an older startup `/auth/me` rejection could clear a newer successful login, and a 401 from a request started under the previous session could dispatch global logout after the new login. `AuthProvider` now ignores startup responses when its auth revision has changed; `apiRequest` dispatches `auth:unauthorized` only when the request began under the still-current session revision. Focused regressions passed **2 files/9 tests** after failing before the fix. Full frontend tests passed **50 files/334 tests**, ESLint, TypeScript/Vite build and diff check passed. ## 2026-10-27 main Roster atomic template update checkpoint

Audited the backend shift-template and availability-template update paths. Both previously performed a version-checked `updateMany`, then fetched the row in a separate query; a subsequent writer could change the row between those steps, causing the first request to return someone else's edit. Both now use one Prisma `update` with ID, store ID and expected version in its condition, returning the row from that same write. Prisma `P2025` remains a 409 stale-version response; duplicate-name `P2002` is now a 409 with a specific message. Three focused unit regressions failed on the old implementation and passed after the change; a fourth covers duplicate names. The backend Calendar read previously queried period rows, shifts and store timezone separately, then sometimes fetched a carryover period in a later query. A concurrent shift edit could pair an old period version with a new shift set in one Calendar response. The read now uses one PostgreSQL repeatable-read transaction for all those rows. The unit suite asserts the isolation level; a disposable PostgreSQL concurrency regression paused after the initial period query, committed a new shift and period version from a second connection, then confirmed Calendar returned the original period version and zero shifts while the database held the newer version and one shift. The backend returns period and shift rows from one repeatable-read transaction, but the frontend previously trusted a successful JSON body without runtime validation. The Calendar repository now validates the returned store/date scope, period structure, shift identity/times/break/role and assignment identity before returning the snapshot. Incomplete data fails the existing Calendar load path with a retryable error instead of reaching grouping. The permissive grouping helper remains available for incomplete optimistic objects outside the API boundary.

The new regression covered a partial shift, incomplete assignment, wrong store/date scope, malformed period and a successful HTTP response containing incomplete data. A red malformed-period case failed before the fix. Targeted Calendar response/load tests passed **17/17**; the full frontend suite passed **52 files / 379 tests** before the final period-validation addition, then targeted tests and the TypeScript/Vite build passed again. Focused ESLint passed. A guarded read-only query of the normal local `molly_roster` database found **28 current Shift rows and zero rows failing the new shift/assignment checks**. Normal frontend 5176 returned HTTP 200 and backend 4020 health reported `database=true,evaluateMolly=true`. No normal business data was written. Real target-account browser/device acceptance and the broader main-system audit remain open.

## 2026-10-27 main Roster Staff independent settings-loading checkpoint

Continued the frontend/backend mutation and publication audit. The backend publication path checks draft status/version, employee/role authority, availability and overlapping assignments inside its transaction. On the Staff page, a separate front-end failure was reproduced: the page waited for Availability templates, Roster settings and Evaluate formal jobs in one `Promise.all`. If the optional job read failed or the template list stalled, the already available store timezone was never committed, so the employee Availability read and editing never started. Two red page regressions reproduced both cases. Settings, template list and formal-job catalog now load independently with store-scoped response guards; settings alone unlocks the Availability read, while each optional failure reports its own error. Because a template may now be saved while its initial list read is pending, the successful save advances the template generation so that a late initial response cannot erase the new list; a third regression covers that order.

## 2026-10-27 main Roster Evaluate response-contract checkpoint

Audited the Roster backend's Evaluate client and route boundary against the current Evaluate `/api/health`, `/auth/logout` and store-job contracts. `EvaluateClient.health()` previously considered any HTTP 200 healthy, including an HTML proxy page. The shared JSON reader previously returned `{}` for a malformed successful response; a successful but non-JSON store-job response then surfaced as a client 400 rather than an upstream 503. Red regressions reproduced both outcomes. Health now requires JSON `{ok:true}`; successful unreadable JSON fails as 503, while failed upstream responses retain their status. The store-job route maps an invalid successful payload to 503, and nullable malformed auth/integration bodies are handled without throwing an unrelated TypeError. ## 2026-10-27 main Roster Evaluate metadata validation checkpoint

Continued the front/back data-contract audit at the Evaluate workforce pagination boundary. `integrationBody` previously accepted any version whose first dot-delimited component was `1`; `workforceEmployees` then used `Number(minor) >= 4` to decide whether a pagination revision was mandatory. A red HTTP regression showed that a successful Evaluate payload with `contractVersion: "1.invalid.0"` could still reach the shift creation service (HTTP 201 in the mocked service). The boundary now requires a numeric `1.minor.patch` form with the expected three components, so an invalid minor cannot bypass 1.4 revision checks. The current Evaluate source declares `1.4.0` and emits ISO UTC `generatedAt` values.

The employee envelope had also accepted arbitrary `generatedAt` strings. Staff renders this field through `Intl.DateTimeFormat`, so a malformed value could crash that view. A second red HTTP regression returned HTTP 200 with `generatedAt: "not-a-date"`; the integration boundary now validates its ISO datetime before forwarding any Evaluate envelope. Existing route test fixtures that used the invalid placeholder `"now"` were updated to valid ISO timestamps.

## 2026-10-27 main Roster uncertain grouped-delete checkpoint

Continued the main front/back audit across backend Shift create/update/delete transactions and Calendar's delete reconciliation. Backend `GET /roster/shifts/:shiftId` is scoped to the requested store and returns 404 for an absent concrete Shift. The frontend had a confirmed reasoning defect after a grouped delete returned an uncertain result: if the first Shift ID was gone, it queried only the original date for the other group IDs. A real Publish then advanced the period to PUBLISHED version 3 and the archive count to two. Exact QA listeners 4010/4035/5177 and both isolated schemas/state files were cleaned.
